   313→    layout.add_row(left, right)
   314→
   315→    console.print()
   316→    console.print(logo)
   317→    console.print()
   318→    console.print(Panel(
   319→        layout,
   320→        title=f"[bold #FFD700]Hermes Agent v0.9.0[/]",
   321→        border_style="#CD7F32",
   322→        padding=(0, 2),
   323→    ))
   324→
   325→
   326→# ─── Main playback engine ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   327→
   328→def play_screenplay(path: str, typing_speed: float = 0.04,
   329→                    pause_between: float = 1.0):
   330→    """Load and play a YAML screenplay."""
   331→    with open(path) as f:
   332→        script = yaml.safe_load(f)
   333→
   334→    title = script.get("title", "Hermes Agent Demo")
   335→    scenes = script.get("scenes", [])
   336→
   337→    # Global settings from screenplay
   338→    global_typing_speed = script.get("typing_speed", typing_speed)
   339→    global_pause = script.get("pause_between", pause_between)
   340→
   341→    for item in scenes:
   342→        # Check if it's an action or a conversation scene
   343→        if "action" in item:
   344→            play_action(item, global_typing_speed)
   345→        else:
   346→            scene_speed = item.get("typing_speed", global_typing_speed)
   347→            scene_pause = item.get("pause_between", global_pause)
   348→            play_scene(item, scene_speed, scene_pause)
   349→
   350→    # Final prompt to show it's still "alive"
   351→    show_prompt()
   352→    time.sleep(2)
   353→    sys.stdout.write("\n")
   354→
   355→
   356→# ─── CLI entry point ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   357→
   358→def main():
   359→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Hermes Agent demo player")
   360→    parser.add_argument("script", help="Path to YAML screenplay file")
   361→    parser.add_argument("--typing-speed", type=float, default=0.04,
   362→                        help="Base typing speed in seconds per character")
   363→    parser.add_argument("--pause", type=float, default=1.0,
   364→                        help="Default pause between scenes")
   365→    args = parser.parse_args()
   366→
   367→    try:
   368→        play_screenplay(args.script, args.typing_speed, args.pause)
   369→    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   370→        sys.stdout.write(f"\n{_RST}")
   371→        sys.exit(0)
   372→
   373→
   374→if __name__ == "__main__":
   375→    main()
   376→
```

    76→      import httpx
    77→      from bs4 import BeautifulSoup
    78→
    79→      async def fetch_and_summarize(url: str, max_length: int = 500) -> str:
    80→          async with httpx.AsyncClient() as client:
    81→              resp = await client.get(url, follow_redirects=True)
    82→              resp.raise_for_status()
    83→

    85→          # Extract main content, strip boilerplate
    86→          for tag in soup(["script", "style", "nav", "footer"]):

    88→
    89→          text = soup.get_text(separator=" ", strip=True)
    90→          return text[:max_length] + "..." if len(text) > max_length else text
    91→      ```
    92→
    93→      The function uses `httpx` for async HTTP and `BeautifulSoup` for HTML

```
1→---
     2→name: ascii-video
     3→description: "Production pipeline for ASCII art video — any format. Converts video/audio/images/generative input into colored ASCII character video output (MP4, GIF, image sequence). Covers: video-to-ASCII conversion, audio-reactive music visualizers, generative ASCII art animations, hybrid video+audio reactive, text/lyrics overlays, real-time terminal rendering. Use when users request: ASCII video, text art video, terminal-style video, character art animation, retro text visualization, audio visualizer in ASCII, converting video to ASCII art, matrix-style effects, or any animated ASCII output."
     4→---
     5→
     6→# ASCII Video Production Pipeline
     7→
     8→Full production pipeline for rendering any content as colored ASCII character video.
     9→
    10→## Modes
    11→
    12→| Mode | Input | Output | Read |
    13→|------|-------|--------|------|
    14→| **Video-to-ASCII** | Video file | ASCII recreation of source footage | `references/inputs.md` § Video Sampling |
    15→| **Audio-reactive** | Audio file | Generative visuals driven by audio features | `references/inputs.md` § Audio Analysis |
    16→| **Generative** | None (or seed params) | Procedural ASCII animation | `references/effects.md` |
    17→| **Hybrid** | Video + audio | ASCII video with audio-reactive overlays | Both input refs |
    18→| **Lyrics/text** | Audio + text/SRT | Timed text with visual effects | `references/inputs.md` § Text/Lyrics |
    19→| **TTS narration** | Text quotes + TTS API | Narrated testimonial/quote video with typed text | `references/inputs.md` § TTS Integration |
    20→
    21→## Stack
    22→
    23→Single self-contained Python script per project. No GPU.
    24→
    25→| Layer | Tool | Purpose |
    26→|-------|------|---------|
    27→| Core | Python 3.10+, NumPy | Math, array ops, vectorized effects |
    28→| Signal | SciPy | FFT, peak detection (audio modes only) |
    29→| Imaging | Pillow (PIL) | Font rasterization, video frame decoding, image I/O |
    30→| Video I/O | ffmpeg (CLI) | Decode input, encode output segments, mux audio, mix tracks |
    31→| Parallel | concurrent.futures / multiprocessing | N workers for batch/clip rendering |
    32→| TTS | ElevenLabs API (or similar) | Generate narration clips for quote/testimonial videos |
    33→| Optional | OpenCV | Video frame sampling, edge detection, optical flow |
    34→
    35→## Pipeline Architecture (v2)
    36→
    37→Every mode follows the same 6-stage pipeline. See `references/architecture.md` for implementation details, `references/scenes.md` for scene protocol, and `references/composition.md` for multi-grid composition and tonemap.
    38→
    39→```
    40→┌─────────┐   ┌──────────┐   ┌───────────┐   ┌──────────┐   ┌─────────┐   ┌────────┐
    41→│ 1.INPUT  │→│ 2.ANALYZE │→│ 3.SCENE_FN │→│ 4.TONEMAP │→│ 5.SHADE  │→│ 6.ENCODE│
    42→│ load src │  │ features  │  │ → canvas   │  │ normalize │  │ post-fx  │  │ → video │
    43→└─────────┘   └──────────┘   └───────────┘   └──────────┘   └─────────┘   └────────┘
    44→```
    45→
    46→1. **INPUT** — Load/decode source material (video frames, audio samples, images, or nothing)
    47→2. **ANALYZE** — Extract per-frame features (audio bands, video luminance/edges, motion vectors)
    48→3. **SCENE_FN** — Scene function renders directly to pixel canvas (`uint8 H,W,3`). May internally compose multiple character grids via `_render_vf()` + pixel blend modes. See `references/composition.md`
    49→4. **TONEMAP** — Percentile-based adaptive brightness normalization with per-scene gamma. Replaces linear brightness multipliers. See `references/composition.md` § Adaptive Tonemap
    50→5. **SHADE** — Apply post-processing `ShaderChain` + `FeedbackBuffer`. See `references/shaders.md`
    51→6. **ENCODE** — Pipe raw RGB frames to ffmpeg for H.264/GIF encoding
    52→
    53→## Creative Direction
    54→
    55→**Every project should look and feel different.** The references provide a vocabulary of building blocks — don't copy them verbatim. Combine, modify, and invent.
    56→
    57→### Aesthetic Dimensions to Vary
    58→
    59→| Dimension | Options | Reference |
    60→|-----------|---------|-----------|
    61→| **Character palette** | Density ramps, block elements, symbols, scripts (katakana, Greek, runes, braille), dots, project-specific | `architecture.md` § Character Palettes |
    62→| **Color strategy** | HSV (angle/distance/time/value mapped), discrete RGB palettes, monochrome, complementary, triadic, temperature | `architecture.md` § Color System |
    63→| **Color tint** | Warm, cool, amber, matrix green, neon pink, sepia, ice, blood, void, sunset | `shaders.md` § Color Grade |
    64→| **Background texture** | Sine fields, noise, smooth noise, cellular/voronoi, video source | `effects.md` § Background Fills |
    65→| **Primary effects** | Rings, spirals, tunnel, vortex, waves, interference, aurora, ripple, fire | `effects.md` § Radial / Wave / Fire |
    66→| **Particles** | Energy sparks, snow, rain, bubbles, runes, binary data, orbits, gravity wells | `effects.md` § Particle Systems |
    67→| **Shader mood** | Retro CRT, clean modern, glitch art, cinematic, dreamy, harsh industrial, psychedelic | `shaders.md` § Design Philosophy |
    68→| **Grid density** | xs(8px) through xxl(40px), mixed per layer | `architecture.md` § Grid System |
    69→| **Font** | Menlo, Monaco, Courier, SF Mono, JetBrains Mono, Fira Code, IBM Plex | `architecture.md` § Font Selection |
    70→| **Mirror mode** | None, horizontal, vertical, quad, diagonal, kaleidoscope | `shaders.md` § Mirror Effects |
    71→| **Transition style** | Crossfade, wipe (directional/radial), dissolve, glitch cut | `shaders.md` § Transitions |
    72→
    73→### Per-Section Variation
    74→
    75→Never use the same config for the entire video. For each section/scene/quote:
    76→- Choose a **different background effect** (or compose 2-3)
    77→- Choose a **different character palette** (match the mood)
    78→- Choose a **different color strategy** (or at minimum a different hue)
    79→- Vary **shader intensity** (more bloom during peaks, more grain during quiet)
    80→- Use **different particle types** if particles are active
    81→
    82→### Project-Specific Invention
    83→
    84→For every project, invent at least one of:
    85→- A custom character palette matching the theme
    86→- A custom background effect (combine/modify existing ones)
    87→- A custom color palette (discrete RGB set matching the brand/mood)
    88→- A custom particle character set
    89→
    90→## Workflow
    91→
    92→### Step 1: Determine Mode and Gather Requirements
    93→
    94→Establish with user:
    95→- **Input source** — file path, format, duration
    96→- **Mode** — which of the 6 modes above
    97→- **Sections** — time-mapped style changes (timestamps → effect names)
    98→- **Resolution** — default 1920x1080 @ 24fps; GIFs typically 640x360 @ 15fps
    99→- **Style direction** — dense/sparse, bright/dark, chaotic/minimal, color palette
   100→- **Text/branding** — easter eggs, overlays, credits, themed character sets
   101→- **Output format** — MP4 (default), GIF, PNG sequence
   102→
   103→### Step 2: Detect Hardware and Set Quality
   104→
   105→Before building the script, detect the user's hardware and set appropriate defaults. See `references/optimization.md` § Hardware Detection.
   106→
   107→```python
   108→hw = detect_hardware()
   109→profile = quality_profile(hw, target_duration, user_quality_pref)
   110→log(f"Hardware: {hw['cpu_count']} cores, {hw['mem_gb']:.1f}GB RAM")
   111→log(f"Render: {profile['vw']}x{profile['vh']} @{profile['fps']}fps, {profile['workers']} workers")
   112→```
   113→
   114→Never hardcode worker counts, resolution, or CRF. Always detect and adapt.
   115→
   116→### Step 3: Build the Script
   117→
   118→Write as a single Python file. Major components:
   119→
   120→1. **Hardware detection + quality profile** — see `references/optimization.md`
   121→2. **Input loader** — mode-dependent; see `references/inputs.md`
   122→3. **Feature analyzer** — audio FFT, video luminance, or pass-through
   123→4. **Grid + renderer** — multi-density character grids with bitmap cache; `_render_vf()` helper for value/hue field → canvas
   124→5. **Character palettes** — multiple palettes chosen per project theme; see `references/architecture.md`
   125→6. **Color system** — HSV + discrete RGB palettes as needed; see `references/architecture.md`
   126→7. **Scene functions** — each returns `canvas (uint8 H,W,3)` directly. May compose multiple grids internally via pixel blend modes. See `references/scenes.md` + `references/composition.md`
   127→8. **Tonemap** — adaptive brightness normalization with per-scene gamma; see `references/composition.md`
   128→9. **Shader pipeline** — `ShaderChain` + `FeedbackBuffer` per-section config; see `references/shaders.md`
   129→10. **Scene table + dispatcher** — maps time ranges to scene functions + shader/feedback configs; see `references/scenes.md`
   130→11. **Parallel encoder** — N-worker batch clip rendering with ffmpeg pipes
   131→12. **Main** — orchestrate full pipeline
   132→
   133→### Step 4: Handle Critical Bugs
   134→
   135→#### Font Cell Height (macOS Pillow)
   136→
   137→`textbbox()` returns wrong height. Use `font.getmetrics()`:
   138→
   139→```python
   140→ascent, descent = font.getmetrics()
   141→cell_height = ascent + descent  # correct
   142→```
   143→
   144→#### ffmpeg Pipe Deadlock
   145→
   146→Never use `stderr=subprocess.PIPE` with long-running ffmpeg. Redirect to file:
   147→
   148→```python
   149→stderr_fh = open(err_path, "w")
   150→pipe = subprocess.Popen(cmd, stdin=subprocess.PIPE, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=stderr_fh)
   151→```
   152→
   153→#### Brightness — Use `tonemap()`, Not Linear Multipliers
   154→
   155→ASCII on black is inherently dark. This is the #1 visual issue. **Do NOT use linear `* N` brightness multipliers** — they clip highlights and wash out the image. Instead, use the **adaptive tonemap** function from `references/composition.md`:
   156→
   157→```python
   158→def tonemap(canvas, gamma=0.75):
   159→    """Percentile-based adaptive normalization + gamma. Replaces all brightness multipliers."""
   160→    f = canvas.astype(np.float32)
   161→    lo = np.percentile(f, 1)          # black point (1st percentile)
   162→    hi = np.percentile(f, 99.5)       # white point (99.5th percentile)
   163→    if hi - lo < 1: hi = lo + 1
   164→    f = (f - lo) / (hi - lo)
   165→    f = np.clip(f, 0, 1) ** gamma     # gamma < 1 = brighter mids
   166→    return (f * 255).astype(np.uint8)
   167→```
   168→
   169→Pipeline ordering: `scene_fn() → tonemap() → FeedbackBuffer → ShaderChain → ffmpeg`
   170→
   171→Per-scene gamma overrides for destructive effects:
   172→- Default: `gamma=0.75`
   173→- Solarize scenes: `gamma=0.55` (solarize darkens above-threshold pixels)
   174→- Posterize scenes: `gamma=0.50` (quantization loses brightness range)
   175→- Already-bright scenes: `gamma=0.85`
   176→
   177→Additional brightness best practices:
   178→- Dense animated backgrounds — never flat black, always fill the grid
   179→- Vignette minimum clamped to 0.15 (not 0.12)
   180→- Bloom threshold lowered to 130 (not 170) so more pixels contribute to glow
   181→- Use `screen` blend mode (not `overlay`) when compositing dark ASCII layers — overlay squares dark values: `2 * 0.12 * 0.12 = 0.03`
   182→
   183→#### Font Compatibility
   184→
   185→Not all Unicode characters render in all fonts. Validate palettes at init:
   186→```python
   187→for c in palette:
   188→    img = Image.new("L", (20, 20), 0)
   189→    ImageDraw.Draw(img).text((0, 0), c, fill=255, font=font)
   190→    if np.array(img).max() == 0:
   191→        log(f"WARNING: char '{c}' (U+{ord(c):04X}) not in font, removing from palette")
   192→```
   193→
   194→### Step 4b: Per-Clip Architecture (for segmented videos)
   195→
   196→When the video has discrete segments (quotes, scenes, chapters), render each as a separate clip file. This enables:
   197→- Re-rendering individual clips without touching the rest (`--clip q05`)
   198→- Faster iteration on specific sections
   199→- Easy reordering or trimming in post
   200→
   201→```python
   202→segments = [
   203→    {"id": "intro", "start": 0.0, "end": 5.0, "type": "intro"},
   204→    {"id": "q00", "start": 5.0, "end": 12.0, "type": "quote", "qi": 0, ...},
   205→    {"id": "t00", "start": 12.0, "end": 13.5, "type": "transition", ...},
   206→    {"id": "outro", "start": 208.0, "end": 211.6, "type": "outro"},
   207→]
   208→
   209→from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor, as_completed
   210→with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=hw["workers"]) as pool:
   211→    futures = {pool.submit(render_clip, seg, features, path): seg["id"]
   212→               for seg, path in clip_args}
   213→    for fut in as_completed(futures):
   214→        fut.result()
   215→```
   216→
   217→CLI: `--clip q00 t00 q01` to re-render specific clips, `--list` to show segments, `--skip-render` to re-stitch only.
   218→
   219→### Step 5: Render and Iterate
   220→
   221→Performance targets per frame:
   222→
   223→| Component | Budget |
   224→|-----------|--------|
   225→| Feature extraction | 1-5ms |
   226→| Effect function | 2-15ms |
   227→| Character render | 80-150ms (bottleneck) |
   228→| Shader pipeline | 5-25ms |
   229→| **Total** | ~100-200ms/frame |
   230→
   231→**Fast iteration**: render single test frames to check brightness/layout before full render:
   232→```python
   233→canvas = render_single_frame(frame_index, features, renderer)
   234→Image.fromarray(canvas).save("test.png")
   235→```
   236→
   237→**Brightness verification**: sample 5-10 frames across video, check `mean > 8` for ASCII content.
   238→
   239→## References
   240→
   241→| File | Contents |
   242→|------|----------|
   243→| `references/architecture.md` | Grid system, font selection, character palettes (library of 20+), color system (HSV + discrete RGB), `_render_vf()` helper, compositing, v2 effect function contract |
   244→| `references/inputs.md` | All input sources: audio analysis, video sampling, image conversion, text/lyrics, TTS integration (ElevenLabs, voice assignment, audio mixing) |
   245→| `references/effects.md` | Effect building blocks: 12 value field generators (`vf_sinefield` through `vf_noise_static`), 8 hue field generators (`hf_fixed` through `hf_plasma`), radial/wave/fire effects, particles, composing guide |
   246→| `references/shaders.md` | 38 shader implementations (geometry, channel, color, glow, noise, pattern, tone, glitch, mirror), `ShaderChain` class, full `_apply_shader_step()` dispatch, audio-reactive scaling, transitions, tint presets |
   247→| `references/composition.md` | **v2 core**: pixel blend modes (20 modes with implementations), multi-grid composition, `_render_vf()` helper, adaptive `tonemap()`, per-scene gamma, `FeedbackBuffer` with spatial transforms, `PixelBlendStack` |
   248→| `references/scenes.md` | **v2 scene protocol**: scene function contract, `Renderer` class, `SCENES` table structure, `render_clip()` loop, beat-synced cutting, parallel rendering + pickling constraints, 4 complete scene examples, scene design checklist |
   249→| `references/troubleshooting.md` | NumPy broadcasting traps, blend mode pitfalls, multiprocessing/pickling issues, brightness diagnostics, ffmpeg deadlocks, font issues, performance bottlenecks, common mistakes |
   250→| `references/optimization.md` | Hardware detection, adaptive quality profiles (draft/preview/production/max), CLI integration, vectorized effect patterns, parallel rendering, memory management |
   251→

1→<p align="center">
     2→  <img src="assets/banner.png" alt="Hermes Agent" width="100%">
     3→</p>
     4→
     5→# Hermes Agent ⚕
     6→
     7→<p align="center">
     8→  <a href="https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/"><img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/Docs-hermes--agent.nousresearch.com-FFD700?style=for-the-badge" alt="Documentation"></a>
     9→  <a href="https://discord.gg/NousResearch"><img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/Discord-5865F2?style=for-the-badge&logo=discord&logoColor=white" alt="Discord"></a>
    10→  <a href="https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/main/LICENSE"><img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/License-MIT-green?style=for-the-badge" alt="License: MIT"></a>
    11→  <a href="https://nousresearch.com"><img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20by-Nous%20Research-blueviolet?style=for-the-badge" alt="Built by Nous Research"></a>
    12→</p>
    13→
    14→**The self-improving AI agent built by [Nous Research](https://nousresearch.com).** It's the only agent with a built-in learning loop — it creates skills from experience, improves them during use, nudges itself to persist knowledge, searches its own past conversations, and builds a deepening model of who you are across sessions. Run it on a $5 VPS, a GPU cluster, or serverless infrastructure that costs nearly nothing when idle. It's not tied to your laptop — talk to it from Telegram while it works on a cloud VM.
    15→
    16→Use any model you want — [Nous Portal](https://portal.nousresearch.com), [OpenRouter](https://openrouter.ai) (200+ models), [z.ai/GLM](https://z.ai), [Kimi/Moonshot](https://platform.moonshot.ai), [MiniMax](https://www.minimax.io), OpenAI, or your own endpoint. Switch with `hermes model` — no code changes, no lock-in.
    17→
    18→<table>
    19→<tr><td><b>A real terminal interface</b></td><td>Full TUI with multiline editing, slash-command autocomplete, conversation history, interrupt-and-redirect, and streaming tool output.</td></tr>
    20→<tr><td><b>Lives where you do</b></td><td>Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, and CLI — all from a single gateway process. Voice memo transcription, cross-platform conversation continuity.</td></tr>
    21→<tr><td><b>A closed learning loop</b></td><td>Agent-curated memory with periodic nudges. Autonomous skill creation after complex tasks. Skills self-improve during use. FTS5 session search with LLM summarization for cross-session recall. <a href="https://github.com/plastic-labs/honcho">Honcho</a> dialectic user modeling. Compatible with the <a href="https://agentskills.io">agentskills.io</a> open standard.</td></tr>
    22→<tr><td><b>Scheduled automations</b></td><td>Built-in cron scheduler with delivery to any platform. Daily reports, nightly backups, weekly audits — all in natural language, running unattended.</td></tr>
    23→<tr><td><b>Delegates and parallelizes</b></td><td>Spawn isolated subagents for parallel workstreams. Write Python scripts that call tools via RPC, collapsing multi-step pipelines into zero-context-cost turns.</td></tr>
    24→<tr><td><b>Runs anywhere, not just your laptop</b></td><td>Six terminal backends — local, Docker, SSH, Daytona, Singularity, and Modal. Daytona and Modal offer serverless persistence — your agent's environment hibernates when idle and wakes on demand, costing nearly nothing between sessions. Run it on a $5 VPS or a GPU cluster.</td></tr>
    25→<tr><td><b>Research-ready</b></td><td>Batch trajectory generation, Atropos RL environments, trajectory compression for training the next generation of tool-calling models.</td></tr>
    26→</table>
    27→
    28→---
    29→
    30→## Quick Install
    31→
    32→```bash
    33→curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/main/scripts/install.sh | bash
    34→```
    35→
    36→Works on Linux, macOS, and WSL2. The installer handles everything — Python, Node.js, dependencies, and the `hermes` command. No prerequisites except git.
    37→
    38→> **Windows:** Native Windows is not supported. Please install [WSL2](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/wsl/install) and run the command above.
    39→
    40→After installation:
    41→
    42→```bash
    43→source ~/.bashrc    # reload shell (or: source ~/.zshrc)
    44→hermes              # start chatting!
    45→```
    46→
    47→---
    48→
    49→## Getting Started
    50→
    51→```bash
    52→hermes              # Interactive CLI — start a conversation
    53→hermes model        # Choose your LLM provider and model
    54→hermes tools        # Configure which tools are enabled
    55→hermes config set   # Set individual config values
    56→hermes gateway      # Start the messaging gateway (Telegram, Discord, etc.)
    57→hermes setup        # Run the full setup wizard (configures everything at once)
    58→hermes claw migrate # Migrate from OpenClaw (if coming from OpenClaw)
    59→hermes update       # Update to the latest version
    60→hermes doctor       # Diagnose any issues
    61→```
    62→
    63→📖 **[Full documentation →](https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/)**
    64→

    66→

    68→
    69→All documentation lives at **[hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs](https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/)**:
    70→
    71→| Section | What's Covered |

    73→| [Quickstart](https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/getting-started/quickstart) | Install → setup → first conversation in 2 minutes |

Excellent. Now let me create a comprehensive summary document. Based on my exploration, I have a complete picture:

## Summary Report: Demo Directory and Recording Pipeline

I've thoroughly explored the demo directory and related code. Here's the complete understanding:

### 1. WHAT EXISTS TODAY AND ITS LIMITATIONS

#### Demo Files
- **player.py** (376 lines) - A Python screenplay renderer that plays scripted terminal demos
- **record.tape** - A VHS script that records demo playback to GIF/MP4
- **scripts/example.yaml** - Full-featured example screenplay
- **scripts/test_quick.yaml** - Shorter demo for fast iteration
- **output.cast** - asciinema recording (JSON format)
- **output.gif** & **output.mp4** - Final rendered outputs

#### Current Implementation (player.py)
The player is a **pure Python terminal emulator** that renders scripted terminal interactions:

**What it does:**
- Simulates typing with character-by-character animation and variable speed
- Displays gold prompt (`❯ `) and user messages with bullet styling
- Runs an animated braille spinner with kawaii faces and thinking verbs
- Renders tool progress lines with icons, verbs, details, and duration
- Displays agent responses in Rich panels with markdown support
- Supports demo banners showing available tools/skills
- Clear and pause actions for scene control

**Hardcoded elements:**
- ANSI color codes (gold, cornsilk, bronze, amber specific RGB values)
- Spinner frames and kawaii faces array
- Thinking verbs list
- Specific delays and timing (0.12s per spinner frame, 0.04s base typing speed)
- Prompt character (`❯ `)
- Panel styling (HORIZONTALS box, bronze borders)

**What it CAN'T do:**
- Record to video — it only renders to terminal
- Handle actual tool execution (all tools are hardcoded in YAML)
- Work with real terminal sessions (pure simulation)
- Display actual agent runtime behavior dynamically
- No integration with asciinema, ffmpeg, or agg currently

#### Recording Pipeline (Current: VHS-Based)
The actual video recording uses **VHS** (a CLI tool from Charm Bracelet):

**record.tape** contains:
```
Output demo/output.gif
Set FontSize 14, Width 1200, Height 800
Set FontFamily "JetBrains Mono"
Set Theme "Catppuccin Mocha"
Set TypingSpeed 0ms
Set PlaybackSpeed 1
Type "python demo/player.py demo/scripts/example.yaml"
Enter
Sleep 45s
```

**How it works:**
1. VHS launches a real terminal with specified font/size/theme
2. Runs the demo command: `python demo/player.py demo/scripts/example.yaml`
3. Records the terminal session to asciinema format (`output.cast`)
4. Converts the cast file to GIF/MP4 using ffmpeg internally

**Limitations of current pipeline:**
- Tight coupling to VHS tool (must be installed)
- Fixed timing (45s sleep) — doesn't adapt to demo length
- No frame-level control or optimization
- No integration with agg (asciinema gif generator) — ffmpeg used directly by VHS
- Theme must be pre-installed in terminal
- Dependencies: VHS (CLI tool), ffmpeg (underlying), terminal with font/theme support

### 2. THE RECORDING PIPELINE ARCHITECTURE

```
+------------------+
| player.py        |  (runs in real terminal, outputs ANSI)
| screenplay.yaml  |
+------------------+
         ↓
+------------------+
| VHS script       |  Records terminal session
| record.tape      |
+------------------+
         ↓
+------------------+
| asciinema        |  output.cast (JSON with ANSI events + timestamps)
| format output    |
+------------------+
         ↓
+------------------+
| ffmpeg           |  Converts cast to video frames, encodes MP4/GIF
| (via VHS)        |
+------------------+
         ↓
+------------------+
| output.gif       |  640x360 or custom resolution
| output.mp4       |  H.264 encoded
+------------------+
```

**VHS is the orchestrator** that:
- Spins up a terminal emulator with specific font/theme
- Captures all ANSI output as asciinema data
- Invokes ffmpeg to render frames and encode output
- Handles timing, resolution, color conversion

**asciinema format** (output.cast):
- JSON header with version, width, height, timestamp, shell info
- Array of `[elapsed_time, "o"|"i", content]` tuples
  - "o" = output (from app)
  - "i" = input (from user)
  - elapsed_time = seconds since start
- Preserves all ANSI escape codes (colors, formatting)

**agg is NOT currently used** — VHS uses ffmpeg directly for final encoding.

### 3. SCREENPLAY YAML SCHEMA

**Global config** (top-level):
```yaml
title: string
typing_speed: float (seconds per character, e.g., 0.04)
pause_between: float (default pause between scenes)
```

**Scene entry** (two types):

**Type 1: Agent scene** (user → tools → response):
```yaml
- user: string                           # User message to type
  thinking_time: float                   # Spinner duration (seconds)
  tools:                                 # Array of tool invocations
    - icon: string (emoji or symbol)
      verb: string (9-char padded)
      detail: string (tool details/args)
      duration: string (display duration, e.g., "2.1s")
      delay: float (pause before next tool)
  response: string (markdown text)      # Agent response text
  response_label: string (default: " ⚕ Hermes ")
  thinking_time: float (spinner duration)
  pre_pause: float (before user input)
  post_pause: float (after response)
  typing_speed: float (per-scene override)
  pause_between: float (per-scene override)
```

**Type 2: Action** (non-conversational):
```yaml
- action: "clear"                        # Clear screen
- action: "pause"
  duration: float
- action: "print"
  text: string
  color: string (Rich format, e.g., "bold red")
- action: "banner"                       # Hermes welcome banner
  model: string
  context: string
  session_id: string
  tools_count: int
  skills_count: int
- action: "type_command"                 # Shell command typing
  command: string
  prefix: string (default: "$ ")
  output: string (command output to display)
```

**What's supported:**
- Markdown in responses (rendered via Rich)
- Variable typing speed per scene
- Per-tool delays for staggered rendering
- Arbitrary timing control via pre/post pauses
- Multiple action types mixed with scenes

### 4. HARDCODED VS CONFIGURABLE

**Hardcoded (in player.py code):**
- All ANSI color codes (gold, cornsilk, bronze, amber hex values)
- Spinner animation frames: `['⠋', '⠙', '⠹', ...]` (braille 10-frame loop)
- Kawaii thinking faces (16 face array)
- Thinking verbs list (15 verbs)
- Spinner frame timing: 0.12s per frame
- Prompt symbol: `❯ `
- Typing variable speed logic:
  - Spaces/tabs: 50% base speed
  - Punctuation: 250% base speed
  - Other: 70-130% (jitter)
- Panel styling: HORIZONTALS box, bronze border
- Spinner update timing and erase behavior
- Tool line format with `┊` separator

**Configurable (in screenplay YAML):**
- All text content (user messages, responses, banners)
- Timing (thinking_time, pre_pause, post_pause, delays per tool)
- Thinking verb and kawaii face (randomly selected each time)
- Tool icons, verbs (padding handled internally), details, duration strings
- Response label (defaults to " ⚕ Hermes " but customizable)
- Global typing speed (0.04 default, overridable per scene)
- Action sequence and ordering
- Banner metadata (model name, context size, session ID, tool/skill counts)

**In record.tape (VHS config):**
- Font (JetBrains Mono)
- Font size (14)
- Terminal dimensions (1200x800)
- Color theme (Catppuccin Mocha)
- Typing speed for command (0ms = instant, overridden by player.py)
- Playback speed (1x)
- Output format (GIF or MP4)
- Recording duration (45s sleep)

### 5. OTHER VIDEO/RECORDING TOOLS IN CODEBASE

Found **one major tool**: `/skills/creative/ascii-video/` — a sophisticated ASCII art video production pipeline:

**Purpose:** Convert any media (video, audio, images, generative) into colored ASCII character video (MP4, GIF, image sequence)

**Stack:**
- Python 3.10+ with NumPy (core)
- SciPy (audio FFT, peak detection)
- Pillow/PIL (font rasterization, image I/O)
- ffmpeg (CLI) for decode/encode
- concurrent.futures for parallel rendering
- OpenCV (optional for optical flow)
- ElevenLabs API (optional for TTS narration)

**6 Pipeline Stages:**
1. INPUT — Load/decode source (video/audio/images/none)
2. ANALYZE — Extract per-frame features (audio bands, luminance, motion)
3. SCENE_FN — Render ASCII canvas via pixel blend modes
4. TONEMAP — Adaptive brightness normalization with gamma
5. SHADE — Post-processing (ShaderChain + FeedbackBuffer)
6. ENCODE — Pipe raw RGB frames to ffmpeg

**Modes:**
- Video-to-ASCII (sample video frames as ASCII)
- Audio-reactive (FFT-driven generative visuals)
- Generative (procedural animation)
- Hybrid (video + audio overlays)
- Lyrics/text (timed text with effects)
- TTS narration (testimonials with typed text)

**Customizable:**
- 20+ character palettes (density ramps, symbols, scripts, braille)
- Color strategies (HSV angle/distance/time, discrete RGB, monochrome)
- 12+ background effects (sine fields, noise, cellular, video source)
- 8+ radial/wave/fire effects
- Particle systems (sparks, snow, rain, runes, orbits)
- 38 shader implementations (geometry, channel, color, glow, glitch, mirror)
- Blend modes (20 pixel blending modes with implementations)
- Per-section variation for visual interest

**Not used for demo recording** — this is for general-purpose ASCII art video generation, not terminal session recording.

### KEY FINDINGS

1. **Demo player is completely simulation-based** — no connection to actual Hermes Agent runtime
2. **Recording relies on VHS + ffmpeg** — player.py runs in a real terminal captured by VHS
3. **asciinema format is preserved** but not used downstream (VHS→ffmpeg, not VHS→agg)
4. **Screenplay schema is minimal but flexible** — simple YAML with timing overrides
5. **Most "realism" is through timing control** — the hardcoded speeds and delays make it feel organic
6. **ascii-video skill exists separately** — a full production tool for ASCII art videos, unrelated to demo recording
7. **No integration between demo player and agent runtime** — these are completely decoupled

### DEPENDENCIES FOR RECORDING

**Required (to run record.tape):**
- Python 3.11+ (Hermes requirement)
- VHS (brew install charmbracelet/tap/vhs)
- ffmpeg (for final encoding)
- Terminal with font support (JetBrains Mono minimum)
- YAML parser (pyyaml, already in Hermes deps)

**Optional but assumed for player.py:**
- Rich (for panel rendering — already in Hermes deps)
- PyYAML (already in Hermes deps)

### FILE PATHS

- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/demo/player.py` — Main screenplay renderer
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/demo/record.tape` — VHS recording script
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/demo/scripts/example.yaml` — Full example screenplay
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/demo/scripts/test_quick.yaml` — Quick test screenplay
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/demo/output.cast` — asciinema recording
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/demo/output.gif` — Rendered GIF (717K)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/demo/output.mp4` — Rendered MP4 (554K)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/skills/creative/ascii-video/` — Separate ASCII art video skill with extensive references

> DEVELOPER

I need to design a structured, extensible demo video framework for the Hermes Agent project. The goal is to produce polished demo videos that look like real agent interactions, with support for camera movements (zoom, pan, crop) and a clean pipeline from screenplay to final video.

## Current State
- `demo/player.py` (376 lines) — monolithic script that plays YAML screenplays in terminal with typing animation, spinner, tool lines, Rich panels
- Recording is manual: asciinema → agg → ffmpeg
- No post-processing, no zoom/crop, no camera control
- Colors/styling hardcoded instead of using the actual skin engine from `hermes_cli/skin_engine.py`
- Works but is a quick hack, not a framework

## Key Requirements
1. **Screenplay-driven** — YAML defines everything: dialog, timing, camera movements
2. **Camera system** — zoom, pan, crop per scene or at arbitrary timestamps
3. **Clean pipeline** — single command: `python -m demo screenplay.yaml -o output.mp4`
4. **Extensible** — easy to add new scene types, effects, overlays
5. **Reuses existing code** — skin engine colors, display.py spinner/tool formatting
6. **High quality output** — render at high res, apply camera in post with ffmpeg

## Available Tools
- Python 3.11+, Rich, PyYAML (already in deps)
- asciinema (installed) for terminal recording
- agg (installed) for cast → GIF/PNG frames
- ffmpeg (installed) for video encoding and filter_complex
- The Hermes skin engine (`hermes_cli/skin_engine.py`) for colors/branding
- `agent/display.py` for spinner frames, kawaii faces, tool line formatting

## Architecture Constraints
- The player renders ANSI to a real terminal — that's how we get faithful output
- Camera movements must happen in post-processing (can't zoom a live terminal)
- Timestamps for camera keyframes can be computed from screenplay timing
- agg can render individual frames from .cast files, or we can use ffmpeg on the GIF

## Key Design Questions
1. How should the module be structured? (package layout)
2. How does the camera system work? (screenplay schema + ffmpeg filter generation)
3. How does the timing system work? (screenplay → timestamps → ffmpeg keyframes)
4. What's the recording pipeline? (orchestration of asciinema → agg/ffmpeg → post-process → final)
5. How do we make scene types extensible?

Please design a concrete implementation plan with:
- Package structure (files and their responsibilities)
- The screenplay YAML schema (complete, with camera directives)
- The recording/rendering pipeline architecture
- The camera/zoom post-processing approach
- CLI interface design
- Implementation order (what to build first)

Keep it practical — this should be buildable in one session, not over-engineered.

1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""Hermes Agent demo player — plays scripted terminal sessions.

    40→KAWAII_THINKING = [

    56→# ─── Typing simulation ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
    57→
    58→def type_text(text: str, speed: float = 0.04, color: str = _CORNSILK):
    59→    """Simulate typing text character by character."""
    60→    for ch in text:
    61→        sys.stdout.write(f"{color}{ch}{_RST}")
    62→        sys.stdout.flush()
    63→        # Variable speed: faster for spaces, slower for punctuation
    64→        if ch in " \t":
    65→            time.sleep(speed * 0.5)
    66→        elif ch in ".,!?;:":
    67→            time.sleep(speed * 2.5)
    68→        else:
    69→            # Add slight jitter
    70→            time.sleep(speed * (0.7 + random.random() * 0.6))
    71→
    72→
    73→def show_prompt(symbol: str = "❯ "):
    74→    """Show the Hermes input prompt."""
    75→    sys.stdout.write(f"\n{_CORNSILK}{symbol}{_RST}")
    76→    sys.stdout.flush()
    77→
    78→
    79→def show_user_message(text: str):
    80→    """Show the submitted user message (gold bullet style)."""
    81→    sys.stdout.write(f"\r\033[K")  # Clear prompt line
    82→    sys.stdout.write(f"{_GOLD}● {_BOLD}{text}{_RST}\n")
    83→    sys.stdout.flush()
    84→

    86→# ─── Spinner animation ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
    87→
    88→class DemoSpinner:
    89→    """Animated braille spinner with kawaii faces."""
    90→
    91→    def __init__(self):
    92→        self.running = False
    93→        self.thread = None
    94→        self.start_time = 0
    95→        self.frame_idx = 0
    96→        self.message = ""
    97→        self.last_len = 0
    98→
    99→    def start(self, message: str = ""):
   100→        self.message = message
   101→        self.running = True
   102→        self.start_time = time.time()
   103→        self.frame_idx = 0
   104→        self.last_len = 0
   105→        self.thread = threading.Thread(target=self._animate, daemon=True)
   106→        self.thread.start()
   107→
   108→    def _animate(self):
   109→        while self.running:
   110→            frame = SPINNER_FRAMES[self.frame_idx % len(SPINNER_FRAMES)]
   111→            elapsed = time.time() - self.start_time
   112→            line = f"  {frame} {self.message} ({elapsed:.1f}s)"
   113→            pad = max(self.last_len - len(line), 0)
   114→            sys.stdout.write(f"\r{line}{' ' * pad}")
   115→            sys.stdout.flush()
   116→            self.last_len = len(line)
   117→            self.frame_idx += 1
   118→            time.sleep(0.12)
   119→
   120→    def update(self, message: str):
   121→        self.message = message
   122→
   123→    def stop(self):
   124→        self.running = False
   125→        if self.thread:
   126→            self.thread.join(timeout=0.5)
   127→        blanks = " " * max(self.last_len + 5, 40)
   128→        sys.stdout.write(f"\r{blanks}\r")
   129→        sys.stdout.flush()
   130→

   135→    """Print a tool progress line matching Hermes format."""

   137→    verb = tool.get("verb", "tool")

   141→    # Pad verb to 9 chars like the real agent

   161→# ─── Scene playback ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   162→
   163→def play_scene(scene: dict, typing_speed: float, pause_between: float):
   164→    """Play a single scene (user message → tools → response)."""
   165→
   166→    user_text = scene.get("user", "")
   167→    tools = scene.get("tools", [])
   168→    response = scene.get("response", "")
   169→    thinking_time = scene.get("thinking_time", 2.0)
   170→    response_label = scene.get("response_label", " ⚕ Hermes ")
   171→    pre_pause = scene.get("pre_pause", 0.5)
   172→    post_pause = scene.get("post_pause", 0.5)
   173→
   174→    # --- Show prompt and type user message ---
   175→    if user_text:
   176→        time.sleep(pre_pause)
   177→        show_prompt()
   178→        time.sleep(0.3)
   179→        type_text(user_text, speed=typing_speed)
   180→        time.sleep(0.6)  # Pause before "submit"
   181→        # "Submit" — show as completed message
   182→        show_user_message(user_text)
   183→        time.sleep(0.3)
   184→
   185→    # --- Spinner (thinking) ---
   186→    spinner = DemoSpinner()
   187→    face = random.choice(KAWAII_THINKING)
   188→    verb = random.choice(THINKING_VERBS)
   189→    spinner.start(f"{face} {verb}...")
   190→
   191→    # Simulate thinking time
   192→    # Change verb partway through for realism
   193→    if thinking_time > 2.0:
   194→        time.sleep(thinking_time * 0.4)
   195→        face2 = random.choice(KAWAII_THINKING)
   196→        verb2 = random.choice(THINKING_VERBS)
   197→        spinner.update(f"{face2} {verb2}...")
   198→        time.sleep(thinking_time * 0.6)
   199→    else:
   200→        time.sleep(thinking_time)
   201→
   202→    spinner.stop()
   203→
   204→    # --- Tool progress lines ---
   205→    for tool in tools:
   206→        show_tool_line(tool)
   207→        tool_delay = tool.get("delay", 0.15)
   208→        time.sleep(tool_delay)
   209→
   210→    # --- Agent response ---
   211→    if response:
   212→        show_response(response, label=response_label)
   213→
   214→    time.sleep(post_pause)
   215→
   216→
   217→def play_action(action: dict, typing_speed: float):
   218→    """Play a non-scene action (pause, clear, text, etc.)."""
   219→    action_type = action.get("action")
   220→
   221→    if action_type == "pause":
   222→        time.sleep(action.get("duration", 1.0))
   223→
   224→    elif action_type == "clear":
   225→        os.system("clear")
   226→
   227→    elif action_type == "print":
   228→        text = action.get("text", "")
   229→        color = action.get("color", "")
   230→        if color:
   231→            console.print(f"[{color}]{text}[/]")
   232→        else:
   233→            print(text)
   234→
   235→    elif action_type == "banner":
   236→        # Print a simplified version of the Hermes banner
   237→        _print_demo_banner(action)
   238→
   239→    elif action_type == "type_command":
   240→        # Type a raw command (not in the agent prompt)
   241→        cmd = action.get("command", "")
   242→        prefix = action.get("prefix", "$ ")
   243→        sys.stdout.write(f"\n{_DIM}{prefix}{_RST}")
   244→        sys.stdout.flush()
   245→        time.sleep(0.3)
   246→        type_text(cmd, speed=typing_speed, color=f"{_BOLD}")
   247→        time.sleep(0.5)
   248→        sys.stdout.write("\n")
   249→        sys.stdout.flush()
   250→        output = action.get("output", "")
   251→        if output:
   252→            for line in output.split("\n"):
   253→                sys.stdout.write(f"{_DIM}{line}{_RST}\n")
   254→                sys.stdout.flush()
   255→                time.sleep(0.05)

   325→
   326→# ─── Main playback engine ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   327→
   328→def play_screenplay(path: str, typing_speed: float = 0.04,
   329→                    pause_between: float = 1.0):
   330→    """Load and play a YAML screenplay."""
   331→    with open(path) as f:
   332→        script = yaml.safe_load(f)
   333→
   334→    title = script.get("title", "Hermes Agent Demo")
   335→    scenes = script.get("scenes", [])
   336→
   337→    # Global settings from screenplay
   338→    global_typing_speed = script.get("typing_speed", typing_speed)
   339→    global_pause = script.get("pause_between", pause_between)
   340→
   341→    for item in scenes:
   342→        # Check if it's an action or a conversation scene
   343→        if "action" in item:
   344→            play_action(item, global_typing_speed)
   345→        else:
   346→            scene_speed = item.get("typing_speed", global_typing_speed)
   347→            scene_pause = item.get("pause_between", global_pause)
   348→            play_scene(item, scene_speed, scene_pause)
   349→
   350→    # Final prompt to show it's still "alive"
   351→    show_prompt()
   352→    time.sleep(2)
   353→    sys.stdout.write("\n")
   354→
   355→
   356→# ─── CLI entry point ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   357→
   358→def main():
   359→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Hermes Agent demo player")
   360→    parser.add_argument("script", help="Path to YAML screenplay file")
   361→    parser.add_argument("--typing-speed", type=float, default=0.04,
   362→                        help="Base typing speed in seconds per character")
   363→    parser.add_argument("--pause", type=float, default=1.0,
   364→                        help="Default pause between scenes")
   365→    args = parser.parse_args()
   366→
   367→    try:
   368→        play_screenplay(args.script, args.typing_speed, args.pause)
   369→    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   370→        sys.stdout.write(f"\n{_RST}")
   371→        sys.exit(0)
   372→
   373→
   374→if __name__ == "__main__":
   375→    main()
   376→
```

```
1→"""Hermes CLI skin/theme engine.
     2→
     3→A data-driven skin system that lets users customize the CLI's visual appearance.
     4→Skins are defined as YAML files in ~/.hermes/skins/ or as built-in presets.
     5→No code changes are needed to add a new skin.
     6→
     7→SKIN YAML SCHEMA
     8→================
     9→
    10→All fields are optional. Missing values inherit from the ``default`` skin.
    11→
    12→.. code-block:: yaml
    13→
    14→    # Required: skin identity
    15→    name: mytheme                         # Unique skin name (lowercase, hyphens ok)
    16→    description: Short description        # Shown in /skin listing
    17→
    18→    # Colors: hex values for Rich markup (banner, UI, response box)
    19→    # These are the default (dark terminal) colors.
    20→    colors:
    21→      banner_border: "#CD7F32"            # Panel border color
    22→      banner_title: "#FFD700"             # Panel title text color
    23→      banner_accent: "#FFBF00"            # Section headers (Available Tools, etc.)
    24→      banner_dim: "#B8860B"               # Dim/muted text (separators, labels)
    25→      banner_text: "#FFF8DC"              # Body text (tool names, skill names)
    26→      ui_accent: "#FFBF00"               # General UI accent
    27→      ui_label: "#4dd0e1"                # UI labels
    28→      ui_ok: "#4caf50"                   # Success indicators
    29→      ui_error: "#ef5350"                # Error indicators
    30→      ui_warn: "#ffa726"                 # Warning indicators
    31→      prompt: "#FFF8DC"                  # Prompt text color
    32→      input_rule: "#CD7F32"              # Input area horizontal rule
    33→      response_border: "#FFD700"         # Response box border (ANSI)
    34→      session_label: "#DAA520"           # Session label color
    35→      session_border: "#8B8682"          # Session ID dim color
    36→
    37→    # Light-mode color overrides (optional). Only keys that need to change.
    38→    # When theme_mode is "light", these replace the corresponding colors above.
    39→    colors_light:
    40→      banner_title: "#B8860B"
    41→      prompt: "#5C4A00"
    42→
    43→    # Spinner: customize the animated spinner during API calls
    44→    spinner:
    45→      waiting_faces:                      # Faces shown while waiting for API
    46→        - "(⚔)"
    47→        - "(⛨)"
    48→      thinking_faces:                     # Faces shown during reasoning
    49→        - "(⌁)"
    50→        - "(<>)"
    51→      thinking_verbs:                     # Verbs for spinner messages
    52→        - "forging"
    53→        - "plotting"
    54→      wings:                              # Optional left/right spinner decorations
    55→        - ["⟪⚔", "⚔⟫"]                  # Each entry is [left, right] pair
    56→        - ["⟪▲", "▲⟫"]
    57→
    58→    # Branding: text strings used throughout the CLI
    59→    branding:
    60→      agent_name: "Hermes Agent"          # Banner title, status display
    61→      welcome: "Welcome message"          # Shown at CLI startup
    62→      goodbye: "Goodbye! ⚕"              # Shown on exit
    63→      response_label: " ⚕ Hermes "       # Response box header label
    64→      prompt_symbol: "❯ "                # Input prompt symbol
    65→      help_header: "(^_^)? Commands"      # /help header text
    66→
    67→    # Tool prefix: character for tool output lines (default: ┊)
    68→    tool_prefix: "┊"
    69→
    70→USAGE
    71→=====
    72→
    73→.. code-block:: python
    74→
    75→    from hermes_cli.skin_engine import get_active_skin, list_skins, set_active_skin
    76→
    77→    skin = get_active_skin()
    78→    print(skin.colors["banner_title"])    # "#FFD700"
    79→    print(skin.get_branding("agent_name"))  # "Hermes Agent"
    80→
    81→    set_active_skin("ares")               # Switch to built-in ares skin
    82→    set_active_skin("mytheme")            # Switch to user skin from ~/.hermes/skins/
    83→
    84→BUILT-IN SKINS
    85→==============
    86→
    87→- ``default`` — Classic Hermes gold/kawaii (the current look)
    88→- ``ares``    — Crimson/bronze war-god theme with custom spinner wings
    89→- ``mono``    — Clean grayscale monochrome
    90→- ``slate``   — Cool blue developer-focused theme
    91→
    92→USER SKINS
    93→==========
    94→
    95→Drop a YAML file in ``~/.hermes/skins/<name>.yaml`` following the schema above.
    96→Activate with ``/skin <name>`` in the CLI or ``display.skin: <name>`` in config.yaml.
    97→"""
    98→
    99→import logging
   100→import os
   101→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
   102→from pathlib import Path
   103→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
   104→
   105→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
   106→
   107→
   108→# =============================================================================
   109→# Skin data structure
   110→# =============================================================================
   111→
   112→@dataclass
   113→class SkinConfig:
   114→    """Complete skin configuration."""
   115→    name: str
   116→    description: str = ""
   117→    colors: Dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
   118→    colors_light: Dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
   119→    spinner: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
   120→    branding: Dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
   121→    tool_prefix: str = "┊"
   122→    banner_logo: str = ""    # Rich-markup ASCII art logo (replaces HERMES_AGENT_LOGO)
   123→    banner_hero: str = ""    # Rich-markup hero art (replaces HERMES_CADUCEUS)
   124→
   125→    def get_color(self, key: str, fallback: str = "") -> str:
   126→        """Get a color value with fallback, respecting the active theme mode.
   127→
   128→        If the active theme mode is "light" and a light-mode override exists
   129→        for this key, return it. Otherwise return the default (dark) color.
   130→        """
   131→        mode = get_theme_mode()
   132→        if mode == "light" and key in self.colors_light:
   133→            return self.colors_light[key]
   134→        return self.colors.get(key, fallback)
   135→
   136→    def get_spinner_list(self, key: str) -> List[str]:
   137→        """Get a spinner list (faces, verbs, etc.)."""
   138→        return self.spinner.get(key, [])
   139→
   140→    def get_spinner_wings(self) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
   141→        """Get spinner wing pairs, or empty list if none."""
   142→        raw = self.spinner.get("wings", [])
   143→        result = []
   144→        for pair in raw:
   145→            if isinstance(pair, (list, tuple)) and len(pair) == 2:
   146→                result.append((str(pair[0]), str(pair[1])))
   147→        return result
   148→
   149→    def get_branding(self, key: str, fallback: str = "") -> str:
   150→        """Get a branding value with fallback."""
   151→        return self.branding.get(key, fallback)
   152→
   153→

   621→
   622→# =============================================================================
   623→# Skin loading and management
   624→# =============================================================================
   625→
   626→_active_skin: Optional[SkinConfig] = None
   627→_active_skin_name: str = "default"
   628→_theme_mode: str = "auto"  # "auto", "light", or "dark"
   629→_resolved_theme_mode: Optional[str] = None  # Cached result of auto-detection
   630→
   631→
   632→def _skins_dir() -> Path:
   633→    """User skins directory."""
   634→    home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   635→    return home / "skins"
   636→
   637→
   638→def _load_skin_from_yaml(path: Path) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   639→    """Load a skin definition from a YAML file."""
   640→    try:
   641→        import yaml
   642→        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   643→            data = yaml.safe_load(f)
   644→        if isinstance(data, dict) and "name" in data:
   645→            return data
   646→    except Exception as e:
   647→        logger.debug("Failed to load skin from %s: %s", path, e)
   648→    return None
   649→
   650→
   651→def _build_skin_config(data: Dict[str, Any]) -> SkinConfig:
   652→    """Build a SkinConfig from a raw dict (built-in or loaded from YAML)."""
   653→    # Start with default values as base for missing keys
   654→    default = _BUILTIN_SKINS["default"]
   655→    colors = dict(default.get("colors", {}))
   656→    colors.update(data.get("colors", {}))
   657→    colors_light = dict(default.get("colors_light", {}))
   658→    colors_light.update(data.get("colors_light", {}))
   659→    spinner = dict(default.get("spinner", {}))
   660→    spinner.update(data.get("spinner", {}))
   661→    branding = dict(default.get("branding", {}))
   662→    branding.update(data.get("branding", {}))
   663→
   664→    return SkinConfig(
   665→        name=data.get("name", "unknown"),
   666→        description=data.get("description", ""),
   667→        colors=colors,
   668→        colors_light=colors_light,
   669→        spinner=spinner,
   670→        branding=branding,
   671→        tool_prefix=data.get("tool_prefix", default.get("tool_prefix", "┊")),
   672→        banner_logo=data.get("banner_logo", ""),
   673→        banner_hero=data.get("banner_hero", ""),
   674→    )
   675→
   676→
   677→def list_skins() -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
   678→    """List all available skins (built-in + user-installed).
   679→
   680→    Returns list of {"name": ..., "description": ..., "source": "builtin"|"user"}.
   681→    """
   682→    result = []
   683→    for name, data in _BUILTIN_SKINS.items():
   684→        result.append({
   685→            "name": name,
   686→            "description": data.get("description", ""),
   687→            "source": "builtin",
   688→        })
   689→
   690→    skins_path = _skins_dir()
   691→    if skins_path.is_dir():
   692→        for f in sorted(skins_path.glob("*.yaml")):
   693→            data = _load_skin_from_yaml(f)
   694→            if data:
   695→                skin_name = data.get("name", f.stem)
   696→                # Skip if it shadows a built-in
   697→                if any(s["name"] == skin_name for s in result):
   698→                    continue
   699→                result.append({
   700→                    "name": skin_name,
   701→                    "description": data.get("description", ""),
   702→                    "source": "user",
   703→                })
   704→
   705→    return result
   706→
   707→
   708→def load_skin(name: str) -> SkinConfig:
   709→    """Load a skin by name. Checks user skins first, then built-in."""
   710→    # Check user skins directory
   711→    skins_path = _skins_dir()
   712→    user_file = skins_path / f"{name}.yaml"
   713→    if user_file.is_file():
   714→        data = _load_skin_from_yaml(user_file)
   715→        if data:
   716→            return _build_skin_config(data)
   717→
   718→    # Check built-in skins
   719→    if name in _BUILTIN_SKINS:
   720→        return _build_skin_config(_BUILTIN_SKINS[name])
   721→
   722→    # Fallback to default
   723→    logger.warning("Skin '%s' not found, using default", name)
   724→    return _build_skin_config(_BUILTIN_SKINS["default"])
   725→
   726→
   727→def get_theme_mode() -> str:
   728→    """Get the resolved theme mode ("light" or "dark").
   729→
   730→    If set to "auto", detects the terminal background on first call and
   731→    caches the result in memory for the session. Falls back to "dark" if
   732→    detection fails.
   733→    """
   734→    global _resolved_theme_mode
   735→    if _theme_mode in ("light", "dark"):
   736→        return _theme_mode
   737→    # Auto-detect
   738→    if _resolved_theme_mode is None:
   739→        try:
   740→            from hermes_cli.colors import detect_terminal_background
   741→            result = detect_terminal_background()
   742→            _resolved_theme_mode = result if result != "unknown" else "dark"
   743→        except Exception:
   744→            _resolved_theme_mode = "dark"
   745→    return _resolved_theme_mode
   746→
   747→
   748→def get_theme_mode_setting() -> str:
   749→    """Get the raw theme_mode setting ("auto", "light", or "dark")."""
   750→    return _theme_mode
   751→
   752→
   753→def set_theme_mode(mode: str) -> None:
   754→    """Set the theme mode ("auto", "light", or "dark")."""
   755→    global _theme_mode, _resolved_theme_mode
   756→    if mode not in ("auto", "light", "dark"):
   757→        mode = "auto"
   758→    _theme_mode = mode
   759→    _resolved_theme_mode = None  # Reset cache so auto re-detects
   760→
   761→
   762→def get_active_skin() -> SkinConfig:
   763→    """Get the currently active skin config (cached)."""
   764→    global _active_skin
   765→    if _active_skin is None:
   766→        _active_skin = load_skin(_active_skin_name)
   767→    return _active_skin
   768→
   769→
   770→def set_active_skin(name: str) -> SkinConfig:
   771→    """Switch the active skin. Returns the new SkinConfig."""
   772→    global _active_skin, _active_skin_name
   773→    _active_skin_name = name
   774→    _active_skin = load_skin(name)
   775→    return _active_skin
   776→
   777→
   778→def get_active_skin_name() -> str:
   779→    """Get the name of the currently active skin."""
   780→    return _active_skin_name
   781→
   782→
   783→def init_skin_from_config(config: dict) -> None:
   784→    """Initialize the active skin and theme mode from CLI config at startup.
   785→
   786→    Call this once during CLI init with the loaded config dict.
   787→    When ``theme_mode`` is ``auto``, detection happens lazily on the first
   788→    ``get_theme_mode()`` call so we always reflect the current terminal.
   789→    """
   790→    display = config.get("display", {})
   791→    skin_name = display.get("skin", "default")
   792→    if isinstance(skin_name, str) and skin_name.strip():
   793→        set_active_skin(skin_name.strip())
   794→    else:
   795→        set_active_skin("default")
   796→
   797→    theme_mode = display.get("theme_mode", "auto")
   798→    if isinstance(theme_mode, str) and theme_mode.strip():
   799→        set_theme_mode(theme_mode.strip())
   800→    else:
   801→        set_theme_mode("auto")
   802→
```

"""CLI presentation -- spinner, kawaii faces, tool preview formatting.

Pure display functions and classes with no AIAgent dependency.
Used by AIAgent._execute_tool_calls for CLI feedback.
"""

import json
import logging
import os
import sys
import threading
import time

# ANSI escape codes for coloring tool failure indicators
_RED = "\033[31m"
_RESET = "\033[0m"

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# =========================================================================
# Skin-aware helpers (lazy import to avoid circular deps)
# =========================================================================

def _get_skin():
    """Get the active skin config, or None if not available."""
    try:
        from hermes_cli.skin_engine import get_active_skin
        return get_active_skin()
    except Exception:
        return None


def get_skin_faces(key: str, default: list) -> list:
    """Get spinner face list from active skin, falling back to default."""
    skin = _get_skin()
    if skin:
        faces = skin.get_spinner_list(key)
        if faces:
            return faces
    return default


def get_skin_verbs() -> list:
    """Get thinking verbs from active skin."""
    skin = _get_skin()
    if skin:
        verbs = skin.get_spinner_list("thinking_verbs")
        if verbs:
            return verbs
    return KawaiiSpinner.THINKING_VERBS


def get_skin_tool_prefix() -> str:
    """Get tool output prefix character from active skin."""
    skin = _get_skin()
    if skin:
        return skin.tool_prefix
    return "┊"


# =========================================================================
# Tool preview (one-line summary of a tool call's primary argument)
# =========================================================================

def _oneline(text: str) -> str:
    """Collapse whitespace (including newlines) to single spaces."""
    return " ".join(text.split())


def build_tool_preview(tool_name: str, args: dict, max_len: int = 40) -> str:
    """Build a short preview of a tool call's primary argument for display."""
    if not args:
        return None
    primary_args = {
        "terminal": "command", "web_search": "query", "web_extract": "urls",
        "read_file": "path", "write_file": "path", "patch": "path",
        "search_files": "pattern", "browser_navigate": "url",
        "browser_click": "ref", "browser_type": "text",
        "image_generate": "prompt", "text_to_speech": "text",
```

   111→
   112→    if tool_name == "session_search":
   113→        query = _oneline(args.get("query", ""))
   114→        return f"recall: \"{query[:25]}{'...' if len(query) > 25 else ''}\""
   115→
   116→    if tool_name == "memory":
   117→        action = args.get("action", "")
   118→        target = args.get("target", "")
   119→        if action == "add":
   120→            content = _oneline(args.get("content", ""))
   121→            return f"+{target}: \"{content[:25]}{'...' if len(content) > 25 else ''}\""
   122→        elif action == "replace":
   123→            return f"~{target}: \"{_oneline(args.get('old_text', '')[:20])}\""
   124→        elif action == "remove":
   125→            return f"-{target}: \"{_oneline(args.get('old_text', '')[:20])}\""
   126→        return action
   127→
   128→    if tool_name == "send_message":
   129→        target = args.get("target", "?")
   130→        msg = _oneline(args.get("message", ""))
   131→        if len(msg) > 20:
   132→            msg = msg[:17] + "..."
   133→        return f"to {target}: \"{msg}\""
   134→
   135→    if tool_name.startswith("rl_"):
   136→        rl_previews = {
   137→            "rl_list_environments": "listing envs",
   138→            "rl_select_environment": args.get("name", ""),
   139→            "rl_get_current_config": "reading config",
   140→            "rl_edit_config": f"{args.get('field', '')}={args.get('value', '')}",
   141→            "rl_start_training": "starting",
   142→            "rl_check_status": args.get("run_id", "")[:16],
   143→            "rl_stop_training": f"stopping {args.get('run_id', '')[:16]}",
   144→            "rl_get_results": args.get("run_id", "")[:16],
   145→            "rl_list_runs": "listing runs",
   146→            "rl_test_inference": f"{args.get('num_steps', 3)} steps",
   147→        }
   148→        return rl_previews.get(tool_name)
   149→
   150→    [REDACTED](tool_name)
   151→    if not key:
   152→        for fallback_key in ("query", "text", "command", "path", "name", "prompt", "code", "goal"):
   153→            if fallback_key in args:
   154→                key = fallback_key
   155→                break
   156→
   157→    if not key or key not in args:
   158→        return None
   159→
   160→    value = args[key]
   161→    if isinstance(value, list):
   162→        value = value[0] if value else ""
   163→
   164→    preview = _oneline(str(value))
   165→    if not preview:
   166→        return None
   167→    if len(preview) > max_len:
   168→        preview = preview[:max_len - 3] + "..."
   169→    return preview
   170→
   171→
   172→# =========================================================================
   173→# KawaiiSpinner
   174→# =========================================================================
   175→
   176→class KawaiiSpinner:
   177→    """Animated spinner with kawaii faces for CLI feedback during tool execution."""
   178→
   179→    SPINNERS = {
   180→        'dots': ['⠋', '⠙', '⠹', '⠸', '⠼', '⠴', '⠦', '⠧', '⠇', '⠏'],
   181→        'bounce': ['⠁', '⠂', '⠄', '⡀', '⢀', '⠠', '⠐', '⠈'],
   182→        'grow': ['▁', '▂', '▃', '▄', '▅', '▆', '▇', '█', '▇', '▆', '▅', '▄', '▃', '▂'],
   183→        'arrows': ['←', '↖', '↑', '↗', '→', '↘', '↓', '↙'],
   184→        'star': ['✶', '✷', '✸', '✹', '✺', '✹', '✸', '✷'],
   185→        'moon': ['🌑', '🌒', '🌓', '🌔', '🌕', '🌖', '🌗', '🌘'],
   186→        'pulse': ['◜', '◠', '◝', '◞', '◡', '◟'],
   187→        'brain': ['🧠', '💭', '💡', '✨', '💫', '🌟', '💡', '💭'],
   188→        'sparkle': ['⁺', '˚', '*', '✧', '✦', '✧', '*', '˚'],
   189→    }
   190→
   191→    KAWAII_WAITING = [
   192→        "(｡◕‿◕｡)", "(◕‿◕✿)", "٩(◕‿◕｡)۶", "(✿◠‿◠)", "( ˘▽˘)っ",
   193→        "♪(´ε` )", "(◕ᴗ◕✿)", "ヾ(＾∇＾)", "(≧◡≦)", "(★ω★)",
   194→    ]
   195→
   196→    KAWAII_THINKING = [
   197→        "(｡•́︿•̀｡)", "(◔_◔)", "(¬‿¬)", "( •_•)>⌐■-■", "(⌐■_■)",
   198→        "(´･_･`)", "◉_◉", "(°ロ°)", "( ˘⌣˘)♡", "ヽ(>∀<☆)☆",
   199→        "٩(๑❛ᴗ❛๑)۶", "(⊙_⊙)", "(¬_¬)", "( ͡° ͜ʖ ͡°)", "ಠ_ಠ",
   200→    ]
   201→
   202→    THINKING_VERBS = [
   203→        "pondering", "contemplating", "musing", "cogitating", "ruminating",
   204→        "deliberating", "mulling", "reflecting", "processing", "reasoning",
   205→        "analyzing", "computing", "synthesizing", "formulating", "brainstorming",
   206→    ]
   207→
   208→    def __init__(self, message: str = "", spinner_type: str = 'dots'):
   209→        self.message = message
   210→        self.spinner_frames = self.SPINNERS.get(spinner_type, self.SPINNERS['dots'])
   211→        self.running = False
   212→        self.thread = None
   213→        self.frame_idx = 0
   214→        self.start_time = None
   215→        self.last_line_len = 0
   216→        self._last_flush_time = 0.0  # Rate-limit flushes for patch_stdout compat
   217→        # Capture stdout NOW, before any redirect_stdout(devnull) from
   218→        # child agents can replace sys.stdout with a black hole.
   219→        self._out = sys.stdout
   220→
   221→    def _write(self, text: str, end: str = '\n', flush: bool = False):
   222→        """Write to the stdout captured at spinner creation time."""
   223→        try:
   224→            self._out.write(text + end)
   225→            if flush:
   226→                self._out.flush()
   227→        except (ValueError, OSError):
   228→            pass
   229→
